# LIVE UPDATE 33 — GLOBAL TRANSDUCTION GEOMETRY TEST 05
# GROTHENDIECK ASSEMBLY OF THE TWO SPINES

## 787. Target

Unify:

```text
dependency spine
=
objects + typed arrows + composition

geometric spine
=
identity + state fibers + Bandwidth + Occupancy +
Resolution + history + transport
```

without flattening either one.

## 788. Dependency category

Define:

```text
C_dep
```

with dependency contexts `U,V,...` as objects and typed admissible dependencies:

```text
f : U -> V
```

as morphisms.

Composition and identity arrows reproduce Recursive Dependency Grammar v0.1.

## 789. State assignment

Assign each dependency context a state groupoid/category:

```text
F(U).
```

Its objects are admissible states over `U`.

Its morphisms are admissible state transformations internal to that context.

For dependency arrow:

```text
f : U -> V
```

assign transport:

```text
F(f) : F(U) -> F(V).
```

Require:

```text
F(id_U)=id
F(g compose f)=F(g) compose F(f).
```

This is the first exact compatibility law joining the two spines.

Variance remains operator-dependent: restriction-style domains may use `C_dep^op`.

## 790. Total object

Construct a Grothendieck-construction-like total category:

```text
T_global = integral_C F.
```

Objects are pairs:

```text
(U,x)
```

where `U` is a dependency context and `x` is a state in `F(U)`.

A morphism:

```text
(U,x) -> (V,y)
```

contains:

```text
dependency arrow f : U -> V
+
compatible state transformation F(f)(x) -> y.
```

This is the desired single formal container.

## 791. Projection

There is a natural projection:

```text
p : T_global -> C_dep

p(U,x)=U.
```

Its fiber over `U` is:

```text
p^-1(U)=F(U).
```

Therefore:

```text
dependency spine = base
geometric spine = fibers.
```

## 792. Nested identity projection

Each fiber can itself carry:

```text
q_U : F(U) -> B_ID(U).
```

So the total architecture has a tower:

```text
actual developmental states
        |
        v
identity orbit/stratum data
        |
        v
dependency context.
```

This preserves the four-level identity hierarchy rather than replacing it.

## 793. Vertical and horizontal motion

Vertical:

```text
(U,x) -> (U,x')
```

keeps dependency context fixed.

Horizontal:

```text
(U,x) -> (V,y)
```

moves along dependency arrow `U -> V`.

Möbius monodromy is primarily vertical in a fixed context.

Cross-context transduction has a horizontal component.

This gives the transport/transduction distinction a structural implementation.

## 794. Bandwidth

Bandwidth becomes the admissible outgoing morphism family from `(U,x)`:

```text
B(U,x)
=
available composable freedom from (U,x).
```

It can contain:

```text
vertical freedom
+
horizontal dependency-transition freedom.
```

A scalar Bandwidth is optional derived metadata.

## 795. Occupancy

Occupancy selects the realized object:

```text
(U,x)
```

or a compatible family/section for distributed systems.

Thus Occupancy simultaneously records:

```text
active dependency context
+
realized state within that context.
```

## 796. Resolution

Define readout functor:

```text
R_R : T_global -> T_R.
```

It may forget:

```text
fine state distinctions
fine dependency distinctions
fine morphism distinctions.
```

For readable dependency category `C_dep,R`, require:

```text
p_R compose R_R
=
r_R compose p.
```

So reading the state and then asking its readable context agrees with reading the dependency context first.

This is a concrete commuting-square constraint.

## 797. Resolution as information loss

A coarse Resolution functor may be:

```text
non-injective on objects
non-faithful on morphisms.
```

Higher Resolution retains more distinctions.

Thus:

```text
Resolution = distinguishable organization
```

gets a direct category-theoretic implementation:

```text
higher Resolution forgets less.
```

## 798. Refinement as lifting

Given coarse readable object:

```text
y in T_R,
```

many fine states may satisfy:

```text
R_R(x)=y.
```

Therefore refinement is not inversion from `y` alone.

It is:

```text
provenance-constrained lifting
through the readout functor.
```

This globally generalizes the earlier RELRIN failure.

Resolution reveals inherited structure; it does not manufacture a lift.

## 799. History

History is one composable path in the total category:

```text
(U_0,x_0)
-> ...
-> (U_n,x_n).
```

Earlier ledgers become projections of this path:

```text
dependency history
identity-orbit history
stratum history
within-context developmental history.
```

So multiple history variables can be derived from one total path.

This is a major simplification.

## 800. Transduction

A transduction is an admissible morphism in `T_global`.

Its two pieces are automatically present:

```text
dependency/base component
+
state/fiber component.
```

Thus the previous:

```text
Tau=(Tau_base,Tau_fiber)
```

is no longer auxiliary notation; it is built into the morphism.

## 801. Closure

Candidate closure:

```text
Cl : T_global -> T_global
```

with:

```text
Cl^2 ~= Cl.
```

Vertical closure stabilizes within a context/identity.

Horizontal closure can select/form a new dependency or identity context.

The exact native closure operator remains unresolved.

## 802. Persistence

For developmental update:

```text
D : T_global -> T_global,
```

persistence requires the relevant identity projection to remain admissibly stable under iteration.

The total state may move through contexts while identity is tested through the formal invariant map.

## 803. Environment

Use the weakest representation first:

```text
F_E(U)
```

where environment parameterizes which state fiber and morphisms are available over `U`.

Only enlarge the dependency base to `(U,E)` if the weaker parameterized architecture fails.

## 804. Algebraic layer

Assign local algebra:

```text
A(U)
```

to dependency context `U`.

Dependency arrows induce algebra maps with domain-appropriate variance.

A state/readout representation:

```text
rho_x : A(U) -> End(V_x)
```

produces local geometric/readable structure.

Thus:

```text
C_dep
-> local algebra
-> representation
-> geometry
-> landscape.
```

## 805. Recursive algebraic geometry

At history step `n`:

```text
A_n
rho_n
transition data_n
```

can update to:

```text
A_(n+1)
rho_(n+1)
transition data_(n+1).
```

If updates respect the indexed dependency structure, natural-transformation language becomes available.

Working meaning:

```text
recursive algebraic geometry
=
history-conditioned evolution of local algebraic models,
representations,
and gluing/transport data.
```

## 806. Earlier branches become special cases

```text
Presheaf/sheaf branch:
contravariant indexed assignment.

Groupoid branch:
choose F(U) to be a groupoid.

Orbit-space branch:
quotient identity-preserving morphisms inside F(U).

Möbius branch:
nontrivial local monodromy inside a fiber.

Algebraic-geometry branch:
local algebra/representation layer over dependency contexts.
```

These are no longer competing global answers.

They are components/special cases of the indexed total architecture.

## 807. Fibration caution

The projection:

```text
p : T_global -> C_dep
```

may eventually be a Grothendieck fibration/opfibration if suitable cartesian/cocartesian lifts exist.

Those lifting axioms have not been established.

Therefore promote:

```text
Grothendieck-construction-like total category
```

but not yet:

```text
canonical Grothendieck fibration.
```

## 808. Global Transduction Object v0.1

Promote candidate:

```text
T_global
```

as:

```text
the total category of dependency-indexed state groupoids/categories.
```

It carries in one formal object:

```text
dependency
state
identity
Bandwidth
Occupancy
Resolution
history
transport/transduction
local algebraic realization.
```

It preserves their different roles through projection/fiber structure.

# LIVE UPDATE 34 — GLOBAL TRANSDUCTION GEOMETRY TEST 06
# CARTESIAN / COCARTESIAN LIFTING

## 814. Selected target

The current total object is:

```text
p : T_global -> C_dep
```

with:

```text
T_global
=
Grothendieck-construction-like total category

C_dep
=
typed dependency category.
```

The next question is:

```text
Do native dependency arrows admit canonical lifts
between state fibers?
```

If yes, the total object may upgrade from a generic indexed total category to a genuine:

```text
fibration
opfibration
or bifibration.
```

This test is purely structural.

---

## 815. Why lifting matters

Suppose:

```text
f : U -> V
```

is a dependency arrow.

Given state:

```text
x in F(U),
```

we need to know whether there is a distinguished state transport over `f`.

For a covariant realization/transport interpretation, this is:

```text
x
|
| lift along f
v
f_! x in F(V).
```

For a contravariant restriction interpretation, the natural direction is:

```text
y in F(V)
|
| pull back along f
v
f^* y in F(U).
```

These are different operator families.

The live project must not force one variance on all dependencies.

---

## 816. Cocartesian lift candidate

For forward realization/transduction arrow:

```text
f : U -> V
```

and state:

```text
x in F(U),
```

candidate cocartesian lift:

```text
lambda_f,x :
(U,x)
->
(V,f_!x)
```

lying over `f`.

Intuitively:

```text
f_!x
=
the most universal admissible forward realization
of x in context V.
```

The lift is cocartesian if every competing arrow over:

```text
g compose f
```

factors uniquely through:

```text
lambda_f,x.
```

This is the formal universal-property test.

---

## 817. Cartesian lift candidate

For restriction/context-inheritance arrow:

```text
f : U -> V
```

and state:

```text
y in F(V),
```

candidate cartesian lift:

```text
rho_f,y :
(U,f^*y)
->
(V,y)
```

lying over `f`.

Interpretation:

```text
f^*y
=
the maximally inherited/restricted state over U
compatible with y over V.
```

The lift is cartesian if every competing arrow into `y` over a factorized base arrow uniquely factors through it.

---

## 818. Operator-family split

The current architecture contains at least two dependency semantics:

```text
RESTRICTION / LOCALIZATION
```

and:

```text
REALIZATION / TRANSDUCTION.
```

Therefore the correct question is not:

```text
Is p a fibration?
```

alone.

It is:

```text
Which typed dependency arrows support cartesian lifts?

Which support cocartesian lifts?
```

This is the first serious typed-lifting architecture.

---

## 819. Localization branch strongly favors cartesian lifts

Earlier Resolution/sheaf work repeatedly used:

```text
parent context
-> inherited local context.
```

That behavior is naturally pullback/restriction-like.

For:

```text
f : U -> V
```

interpreted as inclusion/refinement relation, a state over `V` should admit:

```text
f^* : F(V) -> F(U).
```

Thus the localization branch provides a strong candidate cartesian structure.

Status:

```text
STRUCTURALLY SUPPORTED.
```

Exact native pullback construction remains domain-specific.

---

## 820. Transduction branch strongly favors cocartesian lifts

A forward transduction:

```text
f : U -> V
```

carries organizational state from one dependency context to another.

This is naturally pushforward-like:

```text
f_! : F(U) -> F(V).
```

The total morphism:

```text
(U,x)
->
(V,f_!x)
```

is a cocartesian-lift candidate.

Again:

```text
STRUCTURALLY SUPPORTED.
```

But exact universality must be tested per operator family.

---

## 821. Why arbitrary transport is insufficient

A mere functor:

```text
F(f) : F(U) -> F(V)
```

gives some state map.

A cocartesian lift requires more:

```text
it must be universal among arrows over f.
```

Similarly for cartesian lifts.

Therefore previous functorial compatibility is necessary but not sufficient.

Watcher blocks automatic promotion from indexed category to fibration.

---

## 822. Provenance as part of the lifting condition

A native lift must preserve source ancestry.

For forward lift:

```text
Prov(f_!x)
```

must be generated from:

```text
Prov(x)
+
declared dependency f.
```

No additional ancestry may appear silently.

For restriction lift:

```text
Prov(f^*y)
```

must be inherited from:

```text
Prov(y)
```

through the declared context map.

This makes lifting provenance-aware.

---

## 823. Chirality transport condition

A valid lift must carry chirality according to the domain's admissible transport law.

Forward:

```text
chi(f_!x)
~
tau_f(chi(x)).
```

Restriction:

```text
chi(f^*y)
```

must agree with the restriction of the transported chirality class.

Thus a lift can fail even if the underlying dependency arrow exists.

The state geometry constrains transport.

---

## 824. Bandwidth condition

A candidate forward lift may be algebraically definable but unavailable because Bandwidth does not support the required state transition.

Therefore distinguish:

```text
formal lift
```

from:

```text
currently admissible lift.
```

Let:

```text
Lift_f(x)
```

be the formal candidate family.

Then:

```text
Lift_f^B(x)
subseteq
Lift_f(x)
```

contains Bandwidth-supported lifts.

A canonical operative lift exists only if the admissible family contains a distinguished universal representative.

---

## 825. Occupancy condition

Even when a cocartesian lift exists:

```text
f_!x,
```

Occupancy does not imply the lifted state is realized.

So:

```text
lift existence
!=
lift realization.
```

Occupancy selects whether:

```text
(V,f_!x)
```

is instantiated.

This keeps possibility and realization separate.

---

## 826. Resolution condition

A low Resolution readout may merge multiple distinct lifts.

So an apparently unique lift in:

```text
T_R
```

may correspond to several non-equivalent fine lifts upstairs.

Therefore canonicality must be tested in:

```text
T_global
```

or with provenance sufficient to disambiguate.

Resolution cannot create canonical transport by forgetting competing alternatives.

---

## 827. History condition

History can change admissibility of future lifts:

```text
Lift_f^B(x | H,E).
```

Thus even if the formal indexed category is stationary, the operative fibration may be history/environment-conditioned.

This suggests:

```text
static categorical skeleton
+
dynamic admissibility layer.
```

That distinction is important.

---

## 828. Static vs operative fibration

Define:

```text
p_static : T_static -> C_dep
```

using all formally admissible state transports.

Define:

```text
p_oper(H,E,B)
```

as the currently available substructure after history/environment/Bandwidth constraints.

Then:

```text
p_static
```

may be a fibration/opfibration while:

```text
p_oper
```

temporarily lacks some lifts.

This is a strong candidate architecture.

---

## 829. Why this matters for persistence

If an identity-preserving transport requires a lift that becomes unavailable under changing Bandwidth/environment, persistence corridors can disappear.

Thus persistence failure can arise not because identity algebra changed, but because:

```text
the necessary lift ceased to be admissible.
```

This gives a mathematically precise meaning to:

```text
loss of continuation corridor.
```

---

## 830. Cocartesian composition test

Suppose:

```text
f : U -> V
g : V -> W.
```

If cocartesian lifts exist:

```text
x -> f_!x
```

and:

```text
f_!x -> g_!(f_!x),
```

then the composite should agree, up to canonical isomorphism/equivalence, with:

```text
(g compose f)_! x.
```

Candidate coherence:

```text
g_!(f_!x)
~
(g compose f)_!x.
```

This is the exact transduction-composition condition we want.

---

## 831. Cartesian composition test

Likewise:

```text
f : U -> V
g : V -> W
```

with restriction gives:

```text
f^*(g^*z)
~
(g compose f)^*z.
```

This is the precise locality/inherited-context coherence condition.

So both the earlier localization and transduction branches acquire exact compositional tests.

---

## 832. Bifibration candidate

If the same dependency category supports both:

```text
cartesian restriction lifts
```

and:

```text
cocartesian forward realization lifts,
```

with coherent interaction, then:

```text
p : T_global -> C_dep
```

may behave as a bifibration.

This is structurally appealing because MK43 repeatedly uses:

```text
read/restrict downward
```

and:

```text
realize/transduce forward/upward.
```

Status:

```text
STRONG CANDIDATE.
```

Not yet promoted.

---

## 833. Adjunction pressure

If:

```text
f_! : F(U) -> F(V)
```

and:

```text
f^* : F(V) -> F(U)
```

both exist, ask whether:

```text
f_! dashv f^*.
```

That is:

```text
Hom_V(f_!x,y)
~
Hom_U(x,f^*y).
```

This would relate:

```text
forward realization
```

to:

```text
backward restriction/readback.
```

This is an extremely attractive transduction law.

But no source currently establishes such an adjunction.

Watcher records it as the next-level candidate, not a current result.

---

## 834. Why adjunction would be powerful

An adjunction would say:

```text
ways to realize x forward into y
correspond to
ways y restricts back to x.
```

That is almost exactly the semantic relationship expected between:

```text
transduction
```

and:

```text
readback / inherited restriction.
```

It may also connect to:

```text
projection
translation
inverse/recovery
```

without requiring literal invertibility.

This could be important later.

---

## 835. Möbius branch under lifting

Möbius monodromy is a special case where the base loop returns to the same dependency context:

```text
gamma : U -> U.
```

Its cocartesian/cartesian transport acts within:

```text
F(U).
```

The resulting monodromy:

```text
M_gamma
```

is then the transport induced by a closed base path.

This elegantly embeds the Möbius construction inside the lifting architecture.

---

## 836. Holonomy generalization

If lifts are coherent, every closed dependency path:

```text
gamma
```

can induce transport on the fiber:

```text
M_gamma : F(U) -> F(U).
```

Then the earlier developmental-holonomy branch generalizes from Möbius loops to arbitrary dependency loops.

This is a major consequence.

Candidate:

```text
dependency holonomy
=
fiber-state transformation induced by closed dependency traversal.
```

---

## 837. Curvature question appears

If transport around different homotopic/composable loops produces non-equivalent monodromy, the lifting structure carries nontrivial path dependence.

This is the first point where a meaningful:

```text
curvature-like obstruction
```

can be defined.

Do not import differential curvature yet.

The minimal object is:

```text
failure of path-independent transport.
```

This becomes the next mathematical frontier after lifting.

---

The current source/model architecture does not establish enough exact native operators to prove universal cartesian/cocartesian lift axioms.

However it strongly supports typed lift families:

```text
restriction/localization
-> cartesian candidate
```

Functorial composition, provenance, chirality transport, and admissibility supply concrete lift conditions.

with:

## 840. Strongest compression

```text
A dependency arrow does not automatically move a state.

It defines a lifting problem.

Restriction asks:
what state is inherited backward into the smaller/local context?

Realization asks:
what state is carried forward into the target context?

Bandwidth determines which lifts are currently available.

Occupancy determines which available lift is realized.

Resolution can hide competing lifts but cannot create uniqueness.

History/environment change the operative lifting landscape.

Persistence can fail when an identity-preserving lift corridor disappears.
```

and:

# ADJUNCTION BETWEEN REALIZATION AND RESTRICTION

```text
forward realization / transduction:
f_! : F(U) -> F(V)

backward restriction / readback:
f^* : F(V) -> F(U).
```

```text
f_! dashv f^*
```

and:

## 844. Adjoint condition

For:

```text
f : U -> V,
x in F(U),
y in F(V),
```

the candidate adjunction is:

```text
Hom_V(f_!x, y)
~
Hom_U(x, f^*y).
```

Naturality is required in both `x` and `y`.

This is a much stronger statement than merely having both functors.

---

## 845. Interpretation inside the live architecture

Left side:

```text
Hom_V(f_!x, y)
```

means:

```text
admissible ways the forward realization of x
can map into target state y.
```

Right side:

```text
Hom_U(x, f^*y)
```

means:

```text
admissible ways source state x
maps into the restriction/readback of y.
```

The adjunction says these two description spaces are canonically equivalent.

That would make realization and readback mathematically complementary.

---

## 846. Why inverse equivalence is too strong

We do NOT require:

```text
f^* f_! x = x
```

or:

```text
f_! f^* y = y.
```

Transduction can:

```text
change representation
discard unreadable detail
add target-context structure allowed by declared ancestry
or
collapse distinctions under Resolution.
```

So inverse maps are generally inappropriate.

Adjunction is weaker and better matched to the project.

---

## 847. Unit and counit

If:

```text
f_! dashv f^*,
```

there are natural transformations:

```text
eta_x :
x -> f^* f_! x
```

and:

```text
epsilon_y :
f_! f^* y -> y.
```

Interpretation:

```text
eta
=
compare source state with the readback
of its own forward realization.

epsilon
=
compare a target state with the forward realization
of its inherited/restricted source content.
```

These are immediately useful audit objects.

---

## 848. Unit as round-trip ancestry audit

The unit:

```text
eta_x :
x -> f^*f_!x
```

tests:

```text
What survives forward realization and backward readback?
```

If:

```text
eta_x
```

is an admissible equivalence, then the round trip preserves source identity at the declared level.

If not, the deficit precisely localizes transduction loss.

This is a cleaner replacement for asking whether transduction is invertible.

---

## 849. Counit as target reconstruction audit

The counit:

```text
epsilon_y :
f_!f^*y -> y
```

tests:

```text
How much of target y can be reconstructed
from what y exposes to the source context?
```

If `epsilon_y` is not an equivalence, the target contains organization unavailable under the source restriction.

That difference is not automatically invented information.

It may be target-local structure.

Provenance must decide.

---

## 850. Resolution enters naturally

Suppose readback is Resolution-limited.

Then:

```text
f^*y
```

may retain only distinctions readable in source context `U`.

Therefore:

```text
epsilon_y
```

can fail to be an isomorphism even for a perfectly valid transduction.

This makes adjunction compatible with:

```text
higher target Resolution
than source readback Resolution.
```

Very useful.

---

## 851. Bandwidth enters naturally

Forward realization:

```text
f_!x
```

may require Bandwidth unavailable in the operative state.

Thus distinguish:

```text
formal left adjoint
```

from:

```text
currently executable realization.
```

Likewise `f^*` may be formally defined even if the source context lacks operational access to all target distinctions.

Again:

```text
static adjunction
```

and:

```text
operative availability
```

should be separated.

---

## 852. Occupancy enters after adjunction

Adjunction describes admissible relationship between possible states.

Occupancy determines which actual source and target states are realized.

Therefore:

```text
adjunction
=
structural relation

Occupancy
=
actual instantiation.
```

This avoids overloading adjunction with realization probability or selection.

---

## 853. Chirality compatibility

For an adjunction to be acceptable in MK43, the hom-set correspondence must respect transported chirality class.

Candidate condition:

```text
Phi_f :
Hom_V(f_!x,y)
->
Hom_U(x,f^*y)
```

must preserve the declared chirality-transport provenance.

A purely categorical bijection that scrambles chirality ancestry is not sufficient for the native model.

This is a typed adjunction.

---

## 854. Invariant residue compatibility

Likewise, require the unit/counit to preserve or expose the invariant-residue discrepancy explicitly.

Candidate:

```text
Res(eta_x)
```

records round-trip preserved residue.

```text
Res(epsilon_y)
```

records target-local residue not reconstructible from source restriction.

This could become a direct computational audit channel.

---

## 855. History compatibility

Forward realization followed by backward restriction produces a path:

```text
x
-> f_!x
-> f^*f_!x.
```

Even if:

```text
eta_x
```

is an identity equivalence, history has advanced.

So adjunction is naturally compatible with:

```text
identity recurrence
+
history advance.
```

The round trip need not erase its traversal record.

---

## 856. Möbius specialization

For a closed dependency loop:

```text
f : U -> U,
```

the adjunction becomes:

```text
f_! dashv f^*
```

inside one context.

If the loop is reversible only up to admissible equivalence, the unit/counit can encode the mismatch between:

```text
forward monodromy
```

and:

```text
backward readback.
```

This gives Möbius transport a richer audit than simple `J^2=id`.

---

## 857. Candidate Galois-like reading

At a conceptual level, adjunction creates a relation between:

```text
extension / realization
```

and:

```text
restriction / inherited readback.
```

This resembles extension–restriction adjunctions common in algebra.

The live project should borrow the pattern, not assume any specific classical algebraic instance.

---

## 858. Native-support audit

Do the current project sources establish:

```text
Hom_V(f_!x,y)
~
Hom_U(x,f^*y)
```

for any native operator family?

Current answer:

```text
NO DIRECT SOURCE PROOF.
```

The architecture strongly motivates the relation, but current recovered material gives:

```text
translation
restriction
inheritance
projection
readability
```

without an explicit universal hom-set equivalence.

Therefore a canonical adjunction cannot be promoted as recovered structure.

---

## 859. Weak adjunction candidate

Even without a full natural bijection, we may test a weaker condition:

```text
forward realization is left-universal
relative to the chosen restriction class.
```

Operationally:

```text
every admissible map from x into a restricted target
factors through a preferred forward realization.
```

This is still a universal-property question.

If that fails, no adjunction should be claimed.

---

## 860. Round-trip defect objects

Define candidate defects:

```text
Def_unit(x)
=
difference between x and f^*f_!x
modulo admissible equivalence.

Def_counit(y)
=
difference between f_!f^*y and y
modulo admissible equivalence.
```

These are not scalar distances.

They are typed discrepancy records.

Possible contents:

```text
lost ancestry
lost chirality information
lost invariant residue
Resolution collapse
target-local structure
history increment.
```

---

## 861. Why defect objects are valuable even if adjunction fails

If:

```text
Def_unit = 0
```

but:

```text
Def_counit != 0,
```

then source identity survives round-trip while target carries extra non-readback structure.

If both vanish:

```text
the pair behaves locally like an equivalence
at the audited level.
```

If `Def_unit != 0`:

```text
forward realization/readback loses source identity information.
```

This directly connects to transduction fidelity.

---

## 862. Translation fidelity candidate

The previous project had:

```text
Fid_T
```

as a translation-fidelity idea.

Now define fidelity structurally through unit/counit defects.

Candidate:

```text
Fid_f(x,y)
```

is high when the relevant:

```text
Def_unit
Def_counit
```

are small/trivial in the declared invariant dimensions.

This is a better foundation than one arbitrary scalar similarity score.

---

## 863. Source and target asymmetry

The adjunction architecture naturally explains why transduction can be asymmetric.

The source may embed into the readback of its realization:

```text
x -> f^*f_!x
```

without the target being reconstructible from restriction:

```text
f_!f^*y -> y.
```

Thus:

```text
source preservation
```

and:

```text
target reconstruction
```

are separate questions.

This is highly compatible with boundary translation.

---

## 864. Time-Shell interpretation

At a Time-Shell-like boundary:

```text
f_!
```

can model forward translation into environment-readable organization.

```text
f^*
```

can model what the upstream/source side can recover from the downstream readable state.

Then:

```text
eta
```

audits whether upstream identity survives translation/readback.

```text
epsilon
```

audits whether the readable downstream state contains additional contextual organization not recoverable upstream.

This is an attractive candidate formalization.

Still not source-canonical.

---

## 865. Projection versus restriction

Do not identify:

```text
projection
```

with:

```text
f^*.
```

Projection is a downstream representation operator.

Restriction/readback is the context-change operator returning information to/sourceward context.

A projected state may then be restricted/read back.

The operator taxonomy remains:

```text
PROJ != RESTRICT.
```

---

## 866. Transduction square

The global architecture now supports a useful square:

```text
source x
  | f_!
  v
target f_!x
  | readback f^*
  v
source-side reconstruction f^*f_!x
```

with unit:

```text
eta_x : x -> f^*f_!x.
```

This is the formal round-trip test.

A corresponding target reconstruction uses the counit.

---

## 867. Adjoint-lifting relation

If:

```text
f_! dashv f^*
```

and the total projection admits cocartesian/cartesian lifts corresponding to these functors, then the bifibration candidate strengthens considerably.

This would create:

```text
pushforward
pullback
unit
counit
coherent lift composition.
```

That is a very mature categorical architecture.

But it remains conditional.

---

## 868. Test 07 disposition

```text
GLOBAL TRANSDUCTION GEOMETRY TEST 07:
PARTIAL PASS / ADJUNCTION CANDIDATE
```

Passed conceptually/mathematically:

```text
- adjunction is the correct weaker target than invertibility;
- unit and counit give natural round-trip audits;
- source preservation and target reconstruction separate cleanly;
- Resolution/Bandwidth/Occupancy/history fit without contradiction;
- defect objects provide a practical fidelity framework;

```

Not established:

```text
- native hom-set bijection;
- naturality across all relevant states;
- canonical f_! and f^* for project operator classes;
- triangular identities;
- universal adjunction for Time Shell.
```

---

## 869. Promotion

Promote:

```text
ADJOINT TRANSDUCTION AUDIT v0.1
```

not:

```text
canonical adjunction.
```

Core audit objects:

```text
eta_x
epsilon_y
Def_unit(x)
Def_counit(y).
```

These can be used even before the full adjunction is proved.

---

## 870. Next Question Operator result

The highest-value next question is now:

```text
Can the unit/counit defect architecture define
a non-arbitrary transduction curvature / path-dependence measure
around closed dependency loops?
```

This would connect:

```text
adjunction
lifting
holonomy
history
fidelity
and curvature-like obstruction.
```

That is the next branch.

# LIVE UPDATE 36 — GLOBAL TRANSDUCTION GEOMETRY TEST 08
# CLOSED-LOOP CURVATURE AND PATH-DEPENDENCE OBSTRUCTION

## 872. Selected target

The current architecture has:

```text
dependency category C_dep
total transduction object T_global
typed lifts
monodromy / holonomy
adjoint-style round-trip audits
history-bearing transport.
```

The next question is:

```text
Can we define a non-arbitrary curvature-like obstruction
using only failure of path-independent transport?
```

The aim is explicitly NOT to import differential curvature by analogy.

The minimal target is categorical/pathwise:

```text
two admissible paths
same endpoints
different transported result.
```

---

## 873. Parallel transport candidate

Let:

```text
gamma :
U -> V
```

be a composable dependency path.

Assume the typed lifting architecture assigns a state transport:

```text
P_gamma :
F(U) -> F(V).
```

For composable paths:

```text
gamma_1
gamma_2
```

require coherence:

```text
P_(gamma_2 compose gamma_1)
~
P_gamma2 compose P_gamma1.
```

This is the transport law.

---

## 874. Path-independence test

Take two admissible paths with the same endpoints:

```text
gamma_1 : U -> V
gamma_2 : U -> V.
```

Transport state:

```text
x in F(U)
```

along both:

```text
x_1 = P_gamma1(x)
x_2 = P_gamma2(x).
```

If:

```text
x_1 ~ x_2
```

under the declared admissible-equivalence relation, transport is path-independent at `x`.

If:

```text
x_1 not~ x_2,
```

there is a genuine path-dependence obstruction.

---

## 875. Curvature-like defect

Define:

```text
K(gamma_1,gamma_2;x)
=
Def(
    P_gamma1(x),
    P_gamma2(x)
).
```

`Def` is not a scalar by default.

It is a typed discrepancy record.

Possible fields:

```text
ancestry mismatch
chirality mismatch
invariant-residue mismatch
phase-history mismatch
Resolution-dependent mismatch
environmental difference
closure difference
occupancy difference.
```

This is the first non-arbitrary curvature-like object in the live project.

---

## 876. Flatness candidate

Call transport:

```text
flat at x
```

over a path family when:

```text
K(gamma_1,gamma_2;x)=0
```

for every admissible pair with common endpoints.

Again:

```text
flat
```

here means:

```text
path-independent under the declared identity/readout criterion.
```

It is not yet differential flatness.

---

## 877. Closed-loop form

For closed path:

```text
gamma : U -> U,
```

define holonomy transport:

```text
Hol_gamma
=
P_gamma :
F(U) -> F(U).
```

Then curvature-like defect relative to the trivial path is:

```text
K(gamma;x)
=
Def(
    Hol_gamma(x),
    x
).
```

This unifies:

```text
closed-loop holonomy
+
typed transduction defect.
```

---

## 878. Identity-preserving holonomy

A closed loop can have:

```text
q(Hol_gamma(x)) = q(x)
```

while:

```text
Hol_gamma(x) != x.
```

This means:

```text
no base-identity curvature
```

but potentially:

```text
developmental/history curvature.
```

So curvature-like obstruction must be layered.

---

## 879. Three curvature levels

Define:

```text
K_ID
=
defect in identity-orbit/base class.

K_PRES
=
defect in local presentation/readout class.

K_H
=
defect in history/phase/developmental state.
```

Then possible cases include:

### Type A — identity-flat, history-nontrivial

```text
K_ID = 0
K_PRES = 0
K_H != 0.
```

Identity and presentation close; history remembers the loop.

### Type B — identity-flat, presentation-nontrivial

```text
K_ID = 0
K_PRES != 0.
```

Same identity, different local presentation.

### Type C — identity-curved

```text
K_ID != 0.
```

Closed transport changes identity class.

This is the strongest layered curvature taxonomy yet.

---

## 880. Möbius specialization

The Möbius branch is now a textbook internal example.

One loop:

```text
K_ID = 0
K_PRES != 0
K_H != 0.
```

Two loops:

```text
K_ID = 0
K_PRES = 0
K_H may remain != 0.
```

So Möbius transport is not "curved" at the identity level in this toy model, but it can carry nontrivial presentation/history holonomy.

This cleanly separates topology from identity change.

---

## 881. Adjunction defects enter the curvature audit

For a path composed of forward and backward context changes, use the unit/counit defects:

```text
eta_x :
x -> f^*f_!x

epsilon_y :
f_!f^*y -> y.
```

Then loop defect can include:

```text
Def_unit
Def_counit.
```

This gives a path-dependent round-trip audit without inventing a metric.

---

## 882. Curvature from noncommuting squares

Consider a square:

```text
U --f--> V
|        |
g        h
|        |
v        v
W --k--> Z
```

Transport from `U` to `Z` along:

```text
h compose f
```

versus:

```text
k compose g.
```

Define square defect:

```text
K_square(x)
=
Def(
    P_(h compose f)(x),
    P_(k compose g)(x)
).
```

If zero:

```text
the square commutes at x
```

under admissible equivalence.

If nonzero:

```text
the square carries curvature/path dependence.
```

This is an extremely clean test.

---

## 883. Curvature as failure of a naturality square

If the transport assignments are functorial/natural, many such squares commute.

Curvature-like obstruction can therefore be read as:

```text
failure of expected naturality / path coherence.
```

This is more precise than saying:

```text
the space is bent.
```

For this architecture:

```text
curvature = coherence defect.
```

That is the strongest current formulation.

---

## 884. Dependency curvature versus state curvature

The base dependency category may itself have two distinct paths:

```text
gamma_1 != gamma_2
```

with common endpoints.

Even if they are equivalent in the base, state transport can differ.

Therefore distinguish:

```text
dependency curvature
=
path structure in C_dep

state curvature
=
failure of lifted transport to agree upstairs.
```

The first does not imply the second automatically.

The second is the operative transduction obstruction.

---

## 885. Resolution-dependent curvature

Low Resolution may identify two distinct transported outputs:

```text
R_R(x_1) = R_R(x_2)
```

even when:

```text
x_1 != x_2.
```

So define:

```text
K_R
=
Def(
    R_R(P_gamma1(x)),
    R_R(P_gamma2(x))
).
```

Then:

```text
true curvature may be hidden at low Resolution.
```

This mirrors hidden stratum transitions from earlier passes.

---

## 886. Critical Resolution for curvature visibility

Candidate:

```text
R_crit(K)
```

is the minimal/readout threshold at which the path-dependence defect becomes distinguishable.

Below it:

```text
K_R = 0
```

while upstairs:

```text
K != 0.
```

This gives Resolution a precise role in curvature observability.

---

## 887. Bandwidth and curvature

If one path becomes unavailable because Bandwidth shrinks, then path comparison itself may disappear operationally.

So distinguish:

```text
formal curvature
```

from:

```text
operative curvature.
```

Formal:

```text
compare all mathematically admissible paths.
```

Operative:

```text
compare only currently Bandwidth-supported paths.
```

A system can become operationally path-independent simply because alternative corridors vanish.

Watcher blocks interpreting that as true flatness.

---

## 888. Occupancy and curvature

Curvature is a property of available path transport.

Occupancy determines which path/state is actually realized.

Thus:

```text
curvature
!=
which branch was chosen.
```

A curved transport landscape can have only one occupied path at a given moment.

This distinction remains important.

---

## 889. History-conditioned curvature

Because history can alter:

```text
Bandwidth
environment
phase
basin weighting
available morphisms,
```

the curvature defect may depend on history:

```text
K_Hist(gamma_1,gamma_2;x | H,E).
```

Thus the same formal dependency square can be:

```text
flat under one history/environment
curved under another
```

at the operative level.

This is dynamic curvature.

---

## 890. Curvature and persistence

Identity-level curvature:

```text
K_ID != 0
```

means different admissible paths between the same dependency endpoints can land in different identity classes.

This is a serious persistence hazard.

Candidate interpretation:

```text
persistence requires path-coherent identity transport
over the relevant admissible corridor family.
```

So persistence is not only:

```text
one path preserves identity.
```

It may require:

```text
all relevant admissible continuation paths preserve the same identity class.
```

This strengthens the earlier persistence test.

---

## 891. Curvature and universal coupling

At a purely formal level, if many identity fibers share the same dependency corridors, a curvature defect in the shared transport layer can affect multiple downstream realizations.

This resembles the project's universal-coupling architecture:

```text
shared substrate / shared corridor
-> redistributed perturbation
-> many manifolds affected.
```

This pass does not promote a physical identification.

It only records the structural analogy:

```text
shared transport geometry can create correlated downstream effects.
```

---

## 892. Curvature and invariant residue

The cleanest identity-level curvature test uses the minimal invariant:

```text
I_min
=
(
Anc,
Chi,
Res
).
```

Then:

```text
K_ID(gamma_1,gamma_2;x)
=
Def(
    I_min(P_gamma1(x)),
    I_min(P_gamma2(x))
).
```

If zero:

```text
paths may differ in presentation/history
but not in identity.
```

If nonzero:

```text
the path choice changes organizational identity.
```

This is extremely robust.

---

## 893. Scalar curvature is deliberately deferred

One could define a scalar norm:

```text
||K||
```

after choosing weights on defect components.

Do NOT do this yet.

A scalar would hide which invariant failed.

Keep:

```text
K
```

typed and structured.

Only derive scalar summaries later for domain-specific analytics.

---

## 894. Curvature functor candidate

Let:

```text
Path(U,V)
```

be admissible dependency paths.

Transport assignment:

```text
P :
Path(C_dep) -> Trans(F).
```

Then curvature is the obstruction to `P` factoring through a path-equivalence quotient where equivalent paths should induce equivalent state transport.

This is an elegant abstract statement.

If:

```text
gamma_1 ~path gamma_2
```

but:

```text
P_gamma1 not~ P_gamma2,
```

the obstruction is nonzero.

---

## 895. Flat quotient candidate

If curvature vanishes over a path-equivalence relation, transport factors:

```text
Path(C_dep)
-> Path(C_dep)/~path
-> Trans(F).
```

This means only the coarse path class matters.

If curvature is nonzero, fine path history matters.

This directly links curvature to information density:

```text
flat transport compresses path history
curved transport requires more path information.
```

---

## 896. Information-density consequence

In a flat region:

```text
many dependency paths
-> one transport class.
```

That compresses state evolution.

In a curved region:

```text
different paths
-> different transported states.
```

More history must be retained.

Thus curvature contributes to developmental information density.

This is another exact cross-branch connection.

---

```text
STRONG PASS
```

Passed structurally:

Promote:

```text
TRANSDUCTION CURVATURE v0.1
```

defined by:

```text
K(gamma_1,gamma_2;x)
=
typed defect between transported states
along same-endpoint admissible dependency paths.
```

Promote layered defects:

```text
K_ID
K_PRES
K_H.
```

Promote:

```text
flatness = path-independent transport
under declared admissible equivalence.
```

## 899. Strongest compression

```text
Dependency geometry tells us which paths exist.

Lifting tells us how state moves along a path.

Holonomy tells us what a closed path does.

Adjoint defects tell us what round trips lose.

Curvature tells us whether different routes between the same endpoints
produce the same organizational result.

Persistence requires identity-level path coherence.

Resolution determines whether curvature is visible.

Bandwidth determines which curvature comparisons are operationally available.

History records the path distinctions that curvature makes matter.
```

# LIVE UPDATE 37 — GLOBAL TRANSDUCTION GEOMETRY TEST 09
# NATIVE CONNECTION AS ADMISSIBLE LIFT SELECTION

## 902. Selected target

Transduction Curvature v0.1 is already defined by path dependence:

```text
K(gamma_1,gamma_2;x)
=
Def(P_gamma1(x),P_gamma2(x)).
```

The next question is whether the architecture has a native connection-like object whose job is to choose the local lifts composing into `P_gamma`.

The target is deliberately pre-differential:

```text
connection
=
coherent admissible lift-selection rule.
```

No tangent bundle, one-form, metric, or Levi-Civita structure is assumed.

## 903. Connection candidate

For each typed dependency arrow:

```text
f : U -> V
```

and admissible source state:

```text
x in F(U),
```

let:

```text
Lift_f(x)
```

be the candidate lift family.

Define:

```text
Conn(f,x | R,B,H,E,chi)
```

to select an admissible lift:

```text
lambda_f,x :
(U,x) -> (V,y).
```

Thus the connection is conditioned by the state of the live architecture:

```text
Resolution
Bandwidth
History
Environment
Chirality.
```

## 904. Static connection versus operative connection

Separate:

```text
Conn_static
```

from:

```text
Conn_oper.
```

`Conn_static` acts on the formally admissible categorical skeleton.

`Conn_oper` selects only among lifts currently supported by:

```text
Bandwidth
environment
history
Occupancy constraints
chirality admissibility.
```

This parallels the static/operative lifting distinction from Test 06.

## 905. Horizontal morphisms

A selected lift is called connection-horizontal candidate:

```text
lambda_f,x in Hor_Conn.
```

Vertical morphisms remain state changes inside a fixed dependency context:

```text
(U,x) -> (U,x').
```

The connection therefore provides a horizontal/vertical decomposition at the categorical level.

This is structural, not differential.

## 906. Path transport

For dependency path:

```text
gamma
=
f_n compose ... compose f_1,
```

compose selected horizontal lifts:

```text
P_gamma^Conn
=
lambda_fn compose ... compose lambda_f1.
```

This recovers the transport operator used in Transduction Curvature.

So the earlier curvature object now has a generating mechanism.

## 907. Connection coherence

For composable arrows:

```text
U --f--> V --g--> W,
```

the connection is path-coherent when the selected lift for:

```text
g compose f
```

agrees, up to declared equivalence, with the composite selected lifts:

```text
Conn(g compose f,x)
~
Conn(g,F(f)x) compose Conn(f,x).
```

Failure is a local coherence defect.

This is the pre-differential curvature seed.

## 908. Connection curvature

Define:

```text
K_Conn(f,g;x)
=
Def(
    Conn(g compose f,x),
    Conn(g,F(f)x) compose Conn(f,x)
).
```

More generally, for same-endpoint paths:

```text
K_Conn(gamma_1,gamma_2;x)
=
Def(
    P_gamma1^Conn(x),
    P_gamma2^Conn(x)
).
```

This exactly reproduces Transduction Curvature v0.1.

Therefore:

```text
curvature
=
obstruction to coherent composition of connection-selected lifts.
```

This is the strongest definition yet.

## 909. Flat connection

Candidate:

```text
Conn is flat on path family P
```

when all same-endpoint admissible path transports agree under the declared equivalence:

```text
K_Conn = 0.
```

Again, no differential flatness is claimed.

## 910. Connection is not unique by default

If multiple admissible lifts survive:

```text
Lift_f^B(x)
=
{lambda_1,...,lambda_m},
```

the architecture may not determine a unique connection.

Possible selection data include:

```text
minimal defect
maximal invariant preservation
history continuity
basin preference
environmental compatibility
Occupancy constraint.
```

Do not silently choose one.

A connection is additional structure unless native rules make the selection canonical.

## 911. Watcher selection rule

The weakest defensible native selection criterion is lexicographic:

```text
1. preserve declared ancestry;
2. preserve transported chirality class;
3. preserve invariant residue;
4. satisfy operator typing;
5. satisfy Bandwidth/environment/history admissibility;
6. minimize unsupported additional structure.
```

Only after these constraints may a domain-specific preference select among remaining lifts.

This prevents the connection from becoming an arbitrary optimization engine.

## 912. Connection and Resolution

Resolution must not select a fine lift merely because coarse readout merges alternatives.

So:

```text
Conn
```

acts upstairs.

Then:

```text
R_R
```

reads the selected transport.

A Resolution-conditioned connection is allowed only when Resolution changes actual admissibility/available information in a declared way.

This preserves the no-invented-ancestry rule.

## 913. Connection and Bandwidth

Bandwidth determines which horizontal directions are available:

```text
Hor_Conn^B(U,x)
subseteq
Hor_Conn(U,x).
```

Bandwidth collapse can therefore remove horizontal lifts.

This gives a precise geometric interpretation:

```text
Bandwidth loss
=
loss of horizontal transport directions.
```

## 914. Connection and Occupancy

The connection selects/defines admissible transport structure.

Occupancy determines whether a selected corridor is actually realized.

Therefore:

```text
connection != trajectory.
```

This is analogous to:

```text
road network != vehicle path.
```

but the formal object is categorical.

## 915. Connection and history

History can alter operative lift admissibility:

```text
Conn_oper(...|H_n)
```

and selected transport updates history:

```text
H_n -> H_(n+1).
```

Thus the operative connection can evolve recursively.

Candidate:

```text
Conn_(n+1)
=
Update_Conn(
    Conn_n,
    H_(n+1),
    E_(n+1),
    B_(n+1)
).
```

The static dependency skeleton need not change.

## 916. Dynamic connection

This produces:

```text
static connection law
+
dynamic admissibility mask.
```

A stronger model may allow the connection law itself to evolve.

Use the weaker architecture first.

This keeps recursive geometry controlled.

## 917. Connection and monodromy

For closed base path:

```text
gamma : U -> U,
```

the connection induces:

```text
Hol_Conn(gamma)
=
P_gamma^Conn :
F(U) -> F(U).
```

So Möbius monodromy is now:

```text
holonomy of the connection along the Möbius dependency loop.
```

This is the first full placement of the Möbius branch inside Global Transduction Geometry.

## 918. Connection and adjoint audit

If forward selected lifts are governed by:

```text
f_!
```

and backward restriction by:

```text
f^*,
```

the unit/counit defects test whether the connection's forward/backward transport is coherent with the adjoint candidate.

Thus:

```text
connection
generates transport;

adjoint audit
tests round-trip compatibility;

curvature
tests path compatibility.
```

These are now three distinct jobs.

## 919. Connection and persistence

Persistence requires not merely one horizontal lift preserving identity, but connection-level identity coherence across the relevant corridor family.

Candidate:

```text
for all relevant gamma:
q(P_gamma^Conn(x))
=
q_expected(x,gamma)
```

and for identity-preserving loops:

```text
K_ID(gamma;x)=0.
```

This makes the connection part of the persistence mechanism.

## 920. Connection and stratum crossing

A horizontal lift can be:

```text
fiber/identity preserving
```

or:

```text
base-changing.
```

Therefore classify horizontal morphisms:

```text
Hor_ID
=
identity-preserving horizontal transport

Hor_cross
=
identity/stratum-changing transduction.
```

The connection can select both, but operator typing must declare which class is intended.

## 921. Connection on the stratum graph

At coarse scale, the connection induces allowed transport along:

```text
G_strata.
```

A coarse connection edge must retain witness provenance to lower-level selected lifts.

Therefore the connection also lifts recursively through the abstraction hierarchy.

This matches Recursive Dependency Grammar v0.1.

## 922. Local connection data and gluing

Suppose local dependency regions:

```text
U_i
```

carry local connection rules:

```text
Conn_i.
```

On overlaps:

```text
U_i intersect U_j,
```

require compatibility under transition maps:

```text
Conn_j
~
g_ij Conn_i g_ij^-1
```

schematically.

Failure of compatibility creates a gluing/coherence defect.

This is where sheaf/descent machinery may become necessary.

## 923. Connection descent question

A global connection exists only if local lift-selection rules can be glued coherently.

Thus:

```text
local connections
+
overlap compatibility
-> global connection.
```

This is a genuine descent problem.

The global connection cannot be assumed merely because local lift rules exist.

## 924. Curvature and descent obstruction

Two obstruction types now appear:

```text
transport curvature:
path-composition defect

descent defect:
failure of local connection data to glue.
```

They are related but not identical.

This is an important refinement.

## 925. Connection object v0.1

Promote candidate:

```text
TRANSDUCTION CONNECTION v0.1
```

defined as:

```text
a typed, provenance-preserving,
admissibility-aware selection rule
for horizontal lifts of dependency arrows
into the Global Transduction Object.
```

This is the native categorical connection.

# LIVE UPDATE 38 — GLOBAL TRANSDUCTION GEOMETRY TEST 10
# LOCAL-TO-GLOBAL CONNECTION DESCENT

## 930. Target

Test whether local Transduction Connections can glue into one global connection, while treating the newly supplied chirality material as candidate local admissibility data rather than assuming the identification.

## 931. Local cover and connection data

Let the dependency domain be covered by local contexts:

```text
{U_i}.
```

Each context carries a local state geometry `F(U_i)` and local connection:

```text
Conn_i.
```

On overlaps `U_ij`, descriptions are related by admissible transition maps:

```text
g_ij.
```

The invariant requirement is:

```text
local lift selections represent the same transport law
after admissible change of local presentation.
```

Schematic connection compatibility:

```text
Conn_j ~ g_ij Conn_i g_ij^-1.
```

## 932. Descent cocycle

Require pairwise consistency:

```text
g_ii = id
g_ji = g_ij^-1
```

where appropriate.

On triple overlaps require:

```text
g_ij g_jk g_ki ~ id.
```

Define the descent defect:

```text
D_ijk = g_ij g_jk g_ki.
```

`D_ijk ~ id` means the local transition data close. Otherwise there is a descent obstruction.

## 933. Curvature is not descent failure

Keep two distinct obstruction classes:

```text
TRANSPORT CURVATURE:
local/global transport exists,
but alternative paths disagree.

DESCENT DEFECT:
the local transport architectures
cannot be glued into one coherent global architecture.
```

Thus `K` measures path coherence while `D` measures gluing coherence.

## 934. Chirality-fabric candidate

The supplied chirality material motivates local admissibility objects:

```text
A_chi(U_i).
```

A strong candidate description is:

```text
Chirality Fabric
=
local admissibility objects
+
overlap transition maps
+
cocycle compatibility.
```

Symbolically:

```text
ChiFabric
=
({A_chi(U_i)}, {g_ij}, {D_ijk ~ id}).
```

This is a candidate sheaf/groupoid/bundle-like description, not yet recovered canon.

## 935. Recursive chirality evolution must descend

Let `E_i` denote the local recursive chirality evolution rule. On overlaps require:

```text
g_ij E_i ~ E_j g_ij.
```

Meaning:

```text
evolve then translate
~
translate then evolve.
```

Define:

```text
D_E,ij
=
Def(g_ij E_i, E_j g_ij).
```

A nonzero evolution defect means the local update law is chart/presentation dependent.

## 936. Resolution compatibility

The supplied Resolution framework says deeper Resolution reveals inherited organization rather than manufacturing it.

Therefore readout/refinement must commute with overlap translation:

```text
R_j compose g_ij
~
g_ij,R compose R_i.
```

Define:

```text
D_R,ij
```

as the corresponding defect.

A nonzero `D_R` means Resolution reads the same inherited overlap inconsistently.

## 937. Bandwidth compatibility

Bandwidth constrains available local morphisms/lifts.

On overlaps, after holding environment/history fixed, translated available corridors should agree:

```text
g_ij(Hor_B,i) ~ Hor_B,j.
```

Define:

```text
D_B,ij
```

as the mismatch.

This separates genuine local capacity variation from inconsistent descriptions of the same capacity.

## 938. Occupancy gluing

Local occupied states:

```text
x_i in F(U_i)
```

form a global realized state only when:

```text
x_j ~ g_ij(x_i)
```

on overlaps.

Thus global Occupancy is naturally a compatible family/section rather than necessarily one primitive global coordinate array.

## 939. Global persistence criterion

A globally persistent organization now requires:

```text
1. local identity persistence under admissible transport;
2. overlap compatibility of local identity data;
3. stable descent under recursive evolution/history;
4. no unresolved identity-level curvature on relevant corridors.
```

This is stronger than local persistence alone.

## 940. Genesis self-inheritance and descent

The supplied Genesis master equation describes one recursively self-inheriting field across recursive depth, while the Resolution paper says deeper organization is inherited rather than newly manufactured.

That creates mathematical pressure for compatible restriction/refinement maps between local/depth-indexed descriptions.

Descent is therefore a plausible formal language for expressing self-inheritance without treating each depth as a separate ontology.

## 941. Möbius as minimal descent example

A Möbius band is locally ordinary but globally nonorientable because of its gluing rule.

That gives the key lesson:

```text
globally nontrivial fabric
can have locally simple seeds.
```

So if chirality fabric follows this architecture, globally important information may live in transition functions, not merely in local chirality values.

## 942. Information-density consequence

A large global fabric can be represented by:

```text
local objects
+
transition rules
+
recursive update laws
+
provenance.
```

The realized global structure is reconstructed by gluing.

This supplies another exact mechanism for compact grammar producing a large structured realization.

The transition family `{g_ij}` is a 1-cocycle candidate and `D_ijk` is a higher overlap obstruction candidate.

That requires a precise cover, coefficient object, group/nonabelian structure, and equivalence relation.

The conjugation form of the connection transition suggests nonabelian/groupoid-valued descent may ultimately be more appropriate than ordinary abelian Čech cohomology.

# LIVE UPDATE 39 — COMPONENT SALVAGE AND GLOBAL FABRIC CLASSIFICATION

## Methodological ruling

Legacy mathematics does not have to survive as a whole object.

Decompose inherited objects into independently testable components:

```text
local state object
operator
invariant
transition law
projection/readout
closure rule
history rule
topological constraint
provenance relation
```

Each component receives an independent disposition:

```text
REUSE
ADAPT
SPECIALIZE
GENERALIZE
QUARANTINE
REJECT
```

Whole-object equivalence must be proved, never assumed.

## Component Salvage Protocol v0.1

For any old object `M_old`, preserve:
- source provenance,
- original semantic role,
- mathematical form,
- any transformation applied,
- new role in the live architecture,
- confidence/status.

Distinguish:

```text
FORM_REUSE
SEMANTIC_REUSE
DERIVED_REUSE
ANALOGICAL_ONLY
```

This prevents useful mathematics from being discarded just because its original enclosing theory does not fit.

## Chirality-material salvage

The supplied chirality material can be decomposed into:

```text
seed/orientation
occupancy state
neighbor relation
coupling weights
closure/localization term
recursive projection/update
handedness transport
local admissibility grammar
transition/gluing candidate
```

Candidate placements:

```text
seed/orientation -> local state fiber
admissibility grammar -> allowed-morphism structure
neighbor coupling -> local evolution
closure/localization -> stabilization
recursive projection -> admissibility-preserving update
gluing -> descent architecture
handedness transport -> connection-compatible chirality transport
```

The entire legacy object need not be adopted for these pieces to remain useful.

## Global fabric classification

Let local transition data be `{g_ij}`.

Two gluing systems `{g_ij}` and `{g'_ij}` count as equivalent when there exist local reparameterizations `h_i` such that:

```text
g'_ij ~ h_j g_ij h_i^-1
```

Define the gluing-equivalence class:

```text
[g]
```

This is the first global invariant that survives local coordinate/presentation changes.

## Holonomy classifier

For a closed overlap/dependency loop `gamma`, define:

```text
Hol_g(gamma)
=
ordered product of transition maps along gamma.
```

The conjugacy class:

```text
[Hol_g]
```

is invariant under local reparameterization.

Therefore two fabrics with identical local seeds but inequivalent holonomy data are globally distinct in the current architecture.

## Möbius sanity check

Cylinder and Möbius strip provide the minimal example:

```text
same local strip geometry
different gluing
different loop orientation behavior
different global object
```

This validates the classifier strategy at toy-model level.

## Classifier hierarchy

```text
Level 0:
local seed inventory

Level 1:
gluing-equivalence class [g]

Level 2:
holonomy conjugacy class [Hol_g]

Level 3:
assembled global isomorphism/orbit class

Level 4:
cohomological / characteristic-class-like invariants
only after the coefficient structure is fixed
```

## Separation of roles

Promote the distinction:

```text
fabric class
!=
connection class
!=
curvature class
!=
persistence class
```

The clean order is:

```text
local admissibility
+
gluing class
-> global fabric

global fabric
+
connection
-> transport

transport
-> holonomy / curvature

fabric + connection + operative conditions
-> persistence behavior
```

## Reuse of Genesis accounting components

Useful pieces from the Genesis/persistence accounting can be reused independently:

```text
recursive depth d -> depth/history index
Resolution R_d -> readout/refinement structure
Bandwidth B_d -> available morphism/capacity
Occupancy O_d -> realized state/section
Chirality C_d -> admissibility/orientation structure
Inheritance I_d -> ancestry/continuation constraint
```

The multiplicative equation does not need to be accepted wholesale for these component roles to remain mathematically useful.

## Reuse of recursive chirality evolution components

Likewise:

```text
neighbor set N -> adjacency graph
weights w_n -> typed coupling strengths
lambda -> closure/localization pressure
Pi -> admissibility-preserving projection
chi_t -> evolving local state
```

These can be tested separately even if the full evolution equation later changes.

# LIVE UPDATE 40 — GLOBAL TRANSDUCTION GEOMETRY TEST 12
# HOLONOMY COMPLETENESS AUDIT

## 968. Selected target

The current fabric classifier is:

```text
local admissibility type
+
gluing-equivalence class [g]
+
holonomy conjugacy data [Hol_g].
```

The present test asks:

```text
Can two globally inequivalent fabrics
share the same holonomy conjugacy data?
```

If yes, holonomy is not a complete global classifier.

The weakest invariant should be retained only for the distinctions it actually resolves.

---

## 969. What holonomy remembers

For a chosen connection / transition system and closed loop `gamma`:

```text
Hol(gamma)
```

records the net transport induced by that loop.

Up to local reparameterization, the useful invariant is the conjugacy class:

```text
[Hol].
```

This can detect globally nontrivial loop transport even when every local patch is simple.

Möbius versus cylinder remains the minimal toy example.

---

## 970. What holonomy does not automatically remember

Holonomy data need not uniquely determine:

```text
the total gluing cocycle;
the topology of the assembled object;
local curvature away from sampled loops;
higher obstruction data;
characteristic-class-like information;
disconnected-sector structure;
or the full connection up to isomorphism.
```

Therefore:

```text
same [Hol]
```

does not imply, in full generality:

```text
same global fabric.
```

Holonomy is potentially complete only under additional restrictions.

---

## 971. Restricted completeness regime

A strong restricted case exists conceptually when the object is:

```text
flat,
connected,
with fixed base,
fixed structure group/groupoid,
fixed local fiber type,
and classification taken up to the appropriate gauge equivalence.
```

In such a regime, a representation of the loop/fundamental group into the structure group, modulo conjugacy, may classify the flat transport structure.

This is not promoted globally.

It is recorded as:

```text
HOLONOMY-COMPLETE REGIME CANDIDATE.
```

---

## 972. Failure mode A — nonflat local structure

If the connection has nonzero Transduction Curvature on contractible/small loops, then global loop holonomy alone does not reduce classification to a single representation of coarse loop classes.

Local curvature data matter.

Thus:

```text
nonflat fabric
requires more than coarse holonomy representation.
```

---

## 973. Failure mode B — different gluing with identical observed loop data

Suppose the current readout samples only a subset of loops:

```text
Gamma_obs.
```

Two fabrics may satisfy:

```text
Hol_1(gamma) ~ Hol_2(gamma)
for every gamma in Gamma_obs
```

while differing on unsampled loops or higher overlap data.

Therefore observed holonomy can be incomplete even when full holonomy would separate the objects.

This creates a Resolution dependence in classification.

---

## 974. Failure mode C — same loop action, different local-to-global realization

Two assembled objects can share the same loop-action representation while differing in additional global data not encoded by that representation.

Candidate examples of missing data include:

```text
distinct cocycle classes with the same coarse loop image;
different characteristic-class-like invariants;
different singular/stratified structure;
different extension data;
different higher descent data.
```

The exact missing invariant is category-dependent.

---

## 975. Failure mode D — trivial fundamental loop data

A globally nontrivial object can, in some mathematical settings, have trivial or insufficient first-loop data.

Therefore:

```text
trivial holonomy
```

does not universally imply:

```text
globally trivial fabric.
```

This is a decisive reason not to identify fabric class with holonomy class.

---

## 976. Classifier correction

The hierarchy must be revised:

```text
local seed type
        ↓
transition/gluing class [g]
        ↓
connection class [Conn]
        ↓
holonomy data [Hol]
        ↓
curvature/descent data
        ↓
assembled global isomorphism/orbit class
```

Holonomy is now treated as:

```text
a diagnostic invariant,
not the global identity itself.
```

---

where:

A classifier is meaningless without declaring:

```text
what objects are being classified?
```

## 979. ClassifierScope

Candidate fields:

```text
base_object
local_fiber_type
structure_group_or_groupoid
connection_class_allowed
flatness_requirement
singularity_policy
Resolution/readout
equivalence_relation
history/environment conditioning
```

Then a statement such as:

```text
holonomy is complete
```

is valid only relative to a `ClassifierScope`.

This is promoted as a Watcher constraint.

---

## 980. Resolution-limited classifier

At Resolution `R`, define:

```text
Sig_Fabric^R.
```

Two fabrics may be indistinguishable at `R`:

```text
Sig_1^R = Sig_2^R
```

while distinguishable upstairs:

```text
Sig_1 != Sig_2.
```

Thus fabric classification itself is Resolution-dependent.

This matches the broader doctrine:

```text
readable identity
need not equal full identity.
```

---

## 981. Bandwidth-limited classifier

If current Bandwidth makes some loop/corridor tests inaccessible, the operative classifier sees only:

```text
Gamma_oper
subseteq
Gamma_formal.
```

Therefore a fabric may be operationally indistinguishable from another simply because the necessary discriminating transport is unavailable.

Again:

```text
operative equivalence
!=
formal equivalence.
```

---

## 982. History-sensitive classifier

If the operative connection changes with history/environment:

```text
Conn_oper(H,E),
```

then the currently measurable holonomy/curvature signature can change without changing the underlying static fabric class.

Therefore distinguish:

```text
static fabric signature
```

from:

```text
operative transport signature.
```

This prevents dynamic transport state from being mistaken for global fabric identity.

---

## 983. Minimal complete-classifier strategy

Do not jump directly to characteristic classes.

Use the following escalation:

```text
1. local type;
2. gluing equivalence;
3. holonomy;
4. curvature;
5. descent defects;
6. global isomorphism/orbit test;
7. only then add stronger cohomological/characteristic invariants if unresolved degeneracy remains.
```

This preserves the weakest-first methodology.

---

## 984. API/QMO implication

The QMO should no longer store only:

```text
@holonomy_class
```

as the fabric classifier endpoint.

Add:

```text
@fabric_signature
@classifier_scope
@fabric_equivalence_test
```

and relate:

```text
@global_fabric_classifier
USES
@fabric_signature

@fabric_signature
SCOPED_BY
@classifier_scope.
```

This makes classification queryable rather than implicit.

---

## 985. Library implication

The Library address for a fabric should encode:

```text
local type
gluing metadata
connection metadata
holonomy data
curvature data
descent data
scope
provenance
status
```

under one mathematical address.

This directly realizes the "address -> encoded metadata" architecture.

---

## 988. Strongest compression

```text
Holonomy tells us how loops transport state.

It can distinguish many globally different fabrics.

But holonomy is not the fabric.

A complete classifier depends on what class of objects
and what equivalence relation are being used.

The fabric signature must therefore combine
gluing, connection, holonomy, curvature,
descent, topology/stratification, and declared scope.

Only after those fail should stronger characteristic
or cohomological invariants be added.
```

## 991. New metadata policy

Genesis expanded the Library rule:

```text
Store anything mathematically or conceptually useful downstream.
```

This includes:

```text
formal definitions
theories
models
candidate models
analogies
working metaphors
operator taxonomies
language/terminology
notation
failed paths
counterexamples
audit rules
unresolved questions
derivation notes
classification scopes
salvaged components
research heuristics
```

The Library should prefer:

```text
over-capture now
+
typed metadata
+
downstream filtering later
```

rather than early deletion.

This policy is promoted as:

```text
HIGH-RECALL METADATA CAPTURE v0.1.
```

---

## 992. Why language is first-class metadata

A mature theory develops a controlled language because ordinary words become overloaded.

The live Transduction branch already contains specialized meanings for:

```text
Identity
Orbit
Stratum
Fabric
Connection
Transport
Transduction
Bandwidth
Resolution
Occupancy
Persistence
Closure
Holonomy
Curvature
Descent
Lift
Readback
Admissibility
```

These terms should not remain implicit in prose.

Promote a:

```text
TRANSDUCTION LANGUAGE v0.1
```

as a queryable glossary/semantic contract.

---

## 993. Language entry contract

Each language entry should preserve:

```text
term
canonical_handle
definition
role
operator_type
allowed_synonyms
forbidden_conflations
status
source/provenance
first_live_revision
related_terms
```

Example:

```text
Transport
=
fixed-base motion inside the total transduction object.

Forbidden conflation:
Transport != Transduction.

Transduction
=
a morphism that may include base/identity-context change.
```

This prevents semantic drift across threads.

---

## 994. Theory metadata contract

A theory object is not automatically canon.

Define metadata classes:

```text
THEORY
MODEL
ANALOGY
HEURISTIC
HYPOTHESIS
COUNTEREXAMPLE
FAILED_PATH
LANGUAGE_RULE
AUDIT_RULE
QUESTION
```

Each should carry:

```text
status
scope
support
dependencies
known limitations
what would falsify/reject it
provenance.
```

This makes exploratory reasoning recoverable without pretending it is settled.

---

## 995. Analogy metadata contract

Analogies are useful but dangerous when their scope is forgotten.

Store each analogy with:

```text
source_domain
target_domain
mapping
useful_for
breaks_when
status
```

Example:

```text
Möbius analogy
useful_for:
local/simple presentation + nontrivial global gluing,
identity recurrence with history,
orientation-changing loop transport.

breaks_when:
used as proof that all chirality fabric is literally Möbius.
```

This is promoted as:

```text
TYPED ANALOGY RECORD v0.1.
```

---

## 996. Minimal-signature problem

Current redundant fabric signature:

```text
Sig_Fabric
=
(
    LocalType,
    [g],
    [Conn],
    [Hol],
    K,
    D,
    Top,
    Strata
).
```

Question:

```text
Which entries are primitive,
which are derived,
and which are only required under certain classifier scopes?
```

---

## 997. Dependency audit: [Hol] depends on [Conn] plus loop data

Holonomy is generated by connection transport around closed paths.

Therefore, given:

```text
[Conn]
+
base/path structure
```

one can derive:

```text
[Hol].
```

So `[Hol]` is not generally primitive if the full connection and loop structure are stored.

Result:

```text
[Hol]
=
derived diagnostic metadata
under connection-complete storage.
```

Retain it because it is query-efficient and highly informative.

---

## 998. Dependency audit: K depends on [Conn] plus path comparison and Def

Transduction Curvature is defined by:

```text
K(gamma_1,gamma_2;x)
=
Def(
    P_gamma1^Conn(x),
    P_gamma2^Conn(x)
).
```

Therefore if the connection, path structure, admissible-equivalence rule, and defect semantics are stored, `K` is derivable.

Result:

```text
K
=
derived diagnostic metadata
under connection-complete storage.
```

Again, retain precomputed curvature metadata for query efficiency.

---

## 999. Dependency audit: D depends on local gluing data

Descent defect:

```text
D_ijk
=
g_ij g_jk g_ki.
```

Therefore if `[g]` is stored only as an abstract equivalence class, the actual local defect may not be reconstructible without representative transition data.

This means:

```text
[g]
```

alone is insufficient for operational descent auditing.

Need:

```text
GluingData
=
representatives {g_ij}
+
their equivalence/gauge class [g].
```

This is an important correction.

---

## 1000. Dependency audit: Top is not generally derivable from [Hol]

Previous Test 12 already rejected universal holonomy completeness.

Therefore topological/isomorphism metadata cannot be dropped in general.

However `Top` should be scoped:

```text
Top
=
only the global invariants actually established
for the declared category.
```

Do not populate fictional characteristic classes.

---

## 1001. Dependency audit: Strata belongs to identity geometry, not fabric core

Stratification describes orbit/isotropy and identity-type organization.

It is relevant to global fabric behavior, but may be external to the minimal gluing/connection description.

Therefore distinguish:

```text
CORE FABRIC SIGNATURE
```

from:

```text
IDENTITY-GEOMETRY EXTENSIONS.
```

`Strata` moves to an extension block unless the classifier scope explicitly includes identity stratification.

---

## 1002. Primitive/derived split

Candidate primitive core:

```text
Core_Fabric
=
(
    LocalType,
    GluingData,
    [Conn],
    ClassifierScope
).
```

Derived diagnostics:

```text
Derived_Fabric
=
(
    [Hol],
    K,
    D
).
```

Optional/global extensions:

```text
Extensions_Fabric
=
(
    Top,
    Strata,
    characteristic/cohomological data when established
).
```

This is substantially cleaner.

---

## 1003. Why GluingData is stronger than [g]

For reconstruction and audit, the Library should store:

```text
representative transition maps {g_ij}
```

plus:

```text
their equivalence class [g].
```

The class identifies the object up to local reparameterization.

The representative data allow:

```text
descent checks
evolution compatibility
Resolution compatibility
Bandwidth compatibility
reconstruction.
```

This fits the Library philosophy exactly:

```text
address
-> full metadata beneath it.
```

---

## 1004. Minimal sufficient signature v0.1

Promote candidate:

```text
MinimalFabricSignature
=
(
    LocalType,
    GluingData,
    ConnectionClass,
    ClassifierScope
).
```

with derived cache:

```text
DerivedDiagnostics
=
(
    HolonomyClass,
    CurvatureData,
    DescentData
).
```

and optional extension metadata:

```text
GlobalExtensions
=
(
    TopologyData,
    StratificationData,
    HigherInvariantData
).
```

This is not claimed universally minimal across every mathematical category.

It is minimal relative to the current Transduction architecture.

---

## 1005. Reconstruction criterion

The minimal signature is sufficient only if it can reconstruct or regenerate:

```text
admissible local transport
loop holonomy
path-dependence tests
descent tests
and the declared equivalence comparison
```

within its `ClassifierScope`.

If any cannot be reconstructed, the signature must be expanded.

Thus minimality is operational, not aesthetic.

---

## 1006. Query-cost versus storage-cost distinction

Because the Library is intended to be queried repeatedly, it is rational to store both:

```text
primitive metadata
```

and:

```text
derived caches.
```

So:

```text
minimal sufficient signature
!=
minimal stored metadata.
```

This directly implements Genesis's high-recall rule.

The Library should intentionally store redundant but typed metadata when it reduces future recovery/query cost.

---

The QMO/API should now carry a metadata relation layer for non-canonical research objects.

```text
HEURISTIC_SUGGESTS

AUDIT_CONSTRAINS

QUESTION_TARGETS
```

This allows the reasoning history itself to become queryable.
